# DEFECTS

Register of known gaps. Severity: Critical, High, Medium, Low. The scan output of the discovery script is in `docs/erp/DISCOVERY/DEFECTS.md`.

| ID | Area | Description | Severity | Cause | Owning part | Status | Target part | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-001 | Auth | (Part 02.01: sign-in now exists as AUTH_MODE=iam; what remains is that roles are still the constant below until 02.04, and users are the thin store D-059.) Authentication and roles are a development adapter: `AUTH_MODE=dev-headers` trusts `X-Erp-*` request headers, and role permissions are a constant in `src/server/auth.js`. `config.js` refuses it in staging and production, so the app cannot run in production until IAM exists. **Closed 2026-11-24 (audit AUD-DEF-002):** sign-in is `AUTH_MODE=iam` (02.01), roles and permissions are stored and templated (02.04, `platform/authz/engine.js`), `src/server/auth.js` no longer exists, and the configuration refuses the development headers whenever `NODE_ENV` or `ERP_ENVIRONMENT` is staging or production (D-821). Production is still blocked by D-009 (no host), not by IAM. | High | IAM, roles and permissions tables are parts 02.x | 02.05, 02.07 | Closed | 02.05 | — |
| D-002 | Flags | Flag changes are not maker-checker approved; one SYSADM can change a flag. They are audited (`core_audit_log`) and the reason is mandatory. **Partly resolved (audit AUD-DEF-009, 2026-11-24):** flag changes are maker-checker once the Enterprise Control Center (29.01) is on for the company; with it off, one SYSADM still changes a flag directly (audited, reason required). **Resolved 2026-11-25 (AUD-DEF-009):** in staging and production every flag change waits for an IT administrator (and the CFO or a director for a financial or security flag), never the requester; see DECISIONS.md, "Audit remediation". | Medium | Workflow engine is part 04.01 | 04.01 | Closed | 04.01 | — |
| D-003 | Flags | `company_id` and `project_id` have no foreign keys. | Low | Organisation tables are part 02.02 | 02.02 | Open | 02.02 | — |
| D-004 | UI | Admin page has English labels only, no Hindi, and is not inside the final application shell or navigation registry. The shell here is a minimal bar. | Low | Design-system shell and i18n are parts 03.x | 03.01 | Open | 03.01 | — |
| D-005 | CI | The workflow has run on GitHub for pushes to main (run #4 green except `codeql`). Not yet seen on a pull request; `codeql` is blocked by D-012. | Low |  | 00.03 | Open | 00.03 | — |
| D-006 | API docs | `docs/erp/openapi.yaml` is still written by hand, not generated from the code, so it can drift. A replay snapshot (`tests/contract.test.js`) protects the existing routes, but nothing checks the YAML against the real responses. **Resolved 2026-11-25 (audit AUD-DEF-005):** every declared route is in an OpenAPI file: 3,664 in the hand-written files, the other 1,525 in `docs/erp/routes-openapi.yaml`, generated from the route policy (method, parameters, guard and permission, scope, rate, audit, idempotency, the validator that defines the input). `npm run erp:openapi -- --check` is a regression stage: a route in no file, a documented operation without a route, or a stale generated file fails it. The one stale operation (`GET /hr/lookups/employees`, served by `/core/lookups/{name}`) is corrected. | Low | Needs a schema-driven API layer; deferred | 01.01 | Closed | 01.01 | — |
| D-007 | Performance | Measured with `npm run erp:gen-data` on 100,000 documents and 249,726 lines in SQLite (2026-10-30, one developer machine): list page p95 0.2 ms, project page p95 0.4 ms, record read p95 0.06 ms, list count p95 87 ms, every query uses an index. The target (list p95 500 ms) is met with a wide margin, but this is SQLite on a laptop: the run must be repeated on PostgreSQL and on production hardware, and the filtered count (the slowest query) watched as tables grow. | Low | Engine and hardware differ from production | 00.03 | Open | 00.03 | — |
| D-008 | Flags | The page does not hide itself when `ff.prog.p0002` is OFF, because administrators must still reach it to turn flags back on. The flag is evaluated and shown on the page; gating of navigation entries arrives with the shell. | Low | Navigation shell is part 03.x | 03.01 | Open | 03.01 | — |
| D-009 | Deploy | No staging or production host exists. Deploy, backup, smoke-test and rollback are rehearsed on a directory-based environment on one machine, and production promotion with manual approval is not wired. The server also refuses `NODE_ENV=staging` or `production` until IAM exists (D-001). | High | Hosting target not chosen; depends on D-001 | 00.03, 02.07 | Open | 00.03 | — |
| D-010 | Pipeline | Branch protection cannot be enforced: GitHub states that rulesets are not enforced on a private repository until it moves to a GitHub Team organisation account (checked on 2026-10-30). No ruleset was created, because an unenforced one would look protective and not be. Until then "no direct push to main, one reviewer" (BR-01) is a team habit, not a control. Making the repository public or upgrading the plan fixes it; the command is in `RELEASE.md`. **PACK-W0-01 (2026-11-27):** the repository is public since about 2026-11-26, so rulesets are enforced on the free plan and the blocker is gone. The ruleset for `main` (required checks `verify` and `security`, no force push or deletion, one approving review, admin bypass for pull requests only) is written in `RELEASE.md` §2 and waits for the owner's approval; parts 13–15 were still pushed straight to main. Close with the ruleset's URL once it is applied. | High | Free plan, private repository (now public: waiting for the owner to apply the ruleset) | 00.03 | Open | 00.03 | — |
| D-011 | Backup | Resolved in part 29.06: full backups run daily and snapshots every 15 minutes (scheduled jobs), each verified by integrity check and per-table checksums, encrypted (AES-256-GCM) to the off-site location (BACKUP_OFFSITE_DIR) with its hash checked, with retention, isolated restore tests, DR drills and alerts (`tests/admin-backups.test.js`). What remains (true WAL streaming and a cloud second region) is D-794. | Medium | Hosting and backup service not chosen; full DR is part 29.06 | 29.06 | Closed | 29.06 | — |
| D-012 | Security | CodeQL cannot run: GitHub reports "Code scanning is not enabled for this repository" (a repository setting; private repositories may need GitHub Advanced Security). The job is marked non-blocking and is not a required check. `npm audit` and the secret scan do run in CI (first green run 2026-10-30). The secret scanner is a built-in pattern list, not a full scanner. | Medium | Repository setting not enabled | 00.03 | Open | 00.03 | — |
| D-013 | Release | No release has been cut and no commit carries a `Release-note:` line yet, so user-facing release notes are empty. Coverage threshold is 60 % (target 80 %); client code has no line-coverage gate. | Low | First release not yet decided | 00.03 | Open | 00.03 | — |

Those have no meaning for a static guide. | Low | Reporting framework is part 27.x | 01.01 | Open | 01.01 | — |

| D-028 | Jobs | Jobs run in the web process. A long CPU-bound job would delay requests; there is no separate worker process, no priority queue and no per-type concurrency limit. Re-assigned in 30.08 to 30.10: the container and compose files run the web process per slot; a separate worker container needs the production host and its sizing, which the cut-over part decides. Re-assigned in 30.10 to the steady-state backlog: it needs the production host, its engine or a provider (anti-virus, object store, vault, log and trace collectors, certificates, bank endpoints, production-sized hardware), which exist only at the customer's production cut-over. The go-live page makes the dependency visible: the cut-over runbook's configuration step and the readiness criteria for security, performance and operations are evaluated on the production installation, and the defect is part of the backlog handed over at the end of hypercare (ADR-771). | Medium | Deployment topology is part 30.08 | 30.08 | Open | steady-state | — |

| D-455 | Tests | `tests/punches.test.js` "a morning burst: 2,000 punches in a minute" fails at random with a connection reset in the test client (2,000 requests in flight at once): one pass in three on an idle machine here, two in three on the code of part 10.08. It is independent of the engineering module; it needs a bounded client concurrency or a larger listen backlog in the test. **Fixed in 12.04:** the reset was not the 2,000 requests but a keep-alive race. The client and the test server share one event loop; when the burst kept it busy past the server keep-alive (5 s), the server closed an idle connection as the client reused it. Reproduced 2 times in 6 with coverage (bursts of 5.4 to 5.7 s); `tests/helpers.js` now keeps the server side alive 65 s and 10 runs in 10 pass at the same timings. |  |  |  |  | 10.08 | G3 — Project Baseline Gate |

Closed in 166.1 (F-09): the cause was the planning test data, not the Indian date alone: a schedule from this Monday has its first activity planned to finish on the Friday, so on a Saturday or a Sunday it is rightly a late predecessor. The statutory retention for erasure is eight years (constant), not a parameter per kind of record. Re-assigned in 30.08 to 30.10: further record classes are added with the data owners during UAT. Re-assigned in 30.10 to the steady-state backlog: an enhancement or extension beyond the go-live scope of the last part; it is listed in the backlog handed to steady-state support with the hypercare handover (ADR-771), where it is prioritised with the business owners. | Medium | Each further class needs its owner's fields and trigger; file disposal needs the document service's deletion with its own approval. 30.02 re-targets it to 30.08: further record classes, file disposal and an archive database are operations work on the production environment of 30.08; the reconciliation suite of 30.02 does not change archival. | 29.07 | Open | steady-state | — |
